Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
Accton is the global networking/switch white-box leader 
with business expanding into optics, cooling, and rack 
integration. As of 2Q26, switch made up 52% of total sales, 
followed by networking application (46%), and rest from 
others. Accton was founded in 1988 and went publicly 
listed on Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1995. It has facilities in 
Taiwan, US, Southeast Asia, and Europe. Its headquarter is 
in Zhubei, Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We assign a Buy rating to Accton given its leading position 
in networking/switch white-box market to ride on multi-
year AI uptrend, platform migration, and spec upgrade. We 
expect double-digit% sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-28, driven by 
switch upgrade to 800G/1.6T and more project/client win. 
We see further sales/profit upside from more new clients 
and JDM/rack business.

Price to Book Value 12.1x

### Page 5

Investment Thesis
We initiate coverage of Accton at Buy with a PO of NT$3,200 (implies 50% upside
potential), based on 18x 2028E EPS. We see Accton as the AI networking/switch white-
box leader, alongside Celestica (CLS US; covered by Ruplu Bhattacharya), poised to
benefit from multi-year AI upcycle. Accton is expanding into optical/CPO, liquid cooling,
and rack-level integration business to capture a larger portion of system value in AI.
We forecast Accton to post 37%/42% sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-28, with expanding OPM

(Exhibit 41) on top of sustainable/high GM and ROE (Exhibit 48). We expect Accton’s
biggest switch segment (BofAe: 54-68% of 2026-28E sales; Exhibit 7) to post 52-157%
YoY sales growth in 2026-28, thanks to spec upgrade to 800G/1.6T and broader
product/client base.
Near-term catalysts: monthly sales, new customer/project win, Accton’s 3Q26 results
to be announced before mid-Nov (Exhibit 42), Celestica’s 3Q26 results scheduled on 26
Oct US Time (with any positive implication/read-through to Accton at Celestica’s

earnings call on 27 Oct US Time), and clearer project pipeline with spec upgrade for
ASIC into 2027.
Exhibit 7: We expect the biggest switch segment to contribute 54-68% of sales in 2026-28E

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH
AI platform migration & spec upgrade; more client/project

We project 37%/42% sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-28E, driven by CSPs’ switch upgrade to
800G/1.6T, more project/client win, and rising white-box server/switch adoption among
CSP clients. We highlight Accton’s expanding partnership with its top CSP client in
computing (with continued generation migration), optical, liquid cooling, and rack-level
integration. We also expect another key CSP client to contribute double-digit% sales in
2027, thanks to share re-gain without 800G project delay. We see further sales/profit

upside from more new clients (CSP/enterprise/telco) and JDM/rack business.
Solid BS/CF to support capacity expansion & new business
Accton keeps expanding capacity in Taiwan, Vietnam, Singapore, Malaysia, and US for
existing high-end AI business and new optical/CPO/cooling business. We expect annual
capex of NT$6-7bn in 2026-28E but believe the historical high capex can be supported
by strong profitability, solid net cash position for around two decades, and positive FCF.

Attractive valuation with high sales/EPS growth and ROE
Accton’s valuation and PEG both look attractive vs. peers, given higher sales/EPS growth
and higher ROE (Exhibit 11). We believe its solid fundamentals are supported by the
robust market position as networking/switch white-box leader, long history in
networking/switch industry with proven track records, and ongoing R&D efforts. We
view the Street’s concern about 3Q26 margin pressure (BofA: GM/OPM of 18.7%/14.5%,

vs. consensus of 18.9%/14.8%), due to higher component cost, has been well understood
and reflected in share-price performance (-28% in 3Q26, vs. TAIEX +4%). We see any
correction as a good buying opportunity to capture positive long-term outlook.

### Page 6

Key investment risks 
We view the key upside risks for Accton include:  
• Faster 800G/1.6T deployment by key customers, which may lead to higher sales 
growth and/or better margins; 
• Easing component supply tightness/shortage, such as passive components, memory, 
processors, PCB/CCL/substrate; 
• New client/project win, which may support further sales/margin expansion; 
• Faster progress and development in optical/cooling business, which may drive 
further sales/margin expansion; 
• Faster capacity expansion progress in Taiwan and/or overseas, which may support 
higher sales growth and/or more new project/client win; 
• Favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD), which may help on GM with potential 
non-operating gain.

We view the key downside risks for Accton include:  
• Key customers’ AI product or project delay due to supply chain bottleneck, which 
may lead to slower spec migration to 800G/1.6T; 
• Key customers’ demand push-out due to component supply tightness/shortage, such 
as passive components, memory, processors, PCB/CCL/substrate; 
• Key customers’ demand push-out due to resign in chip/board level; 
• GM pressure due to component cost inflation and/or unfavorable FX trend; 
• Slower capacity expansion progress in Taiwan and/or overseas, which may cap sales 
growth or new project/client win; 
• More intense peer competition, which may lead to market/order share loss; 
• Unfavorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) or macro uncertainty, which may 
weigh on GM with potential non-operating loss.

### Page 7

Valuation: PO at NT$3,200 
Our PO of NT$3,200 is based on 18x 2028E EPS (or around 26x 2027E EPS). In our view, 
the 18x P/E multiple looks justified given Accton’s 37%/42% sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-
28E, expanding OPM, sustainable/high GM and ROE, besides solid position in networking 
switch white box market. 
The 18x target P/E represents around 1SD above Accton’s historical trading average 
since 2008, the year in which the company first became profitable (Exhibit 2), and the 
mid-level of historical P/E range of 2-32x. We believe the above-average P/E multiple 
can be justified as stronger sales/EPS CAGR and higher margins than historical levels. 
18x is below 20x our US analyst Ruplu Bhattacharya used for Celestica (CLS US; the key 
peer to Accton). We made a discount on Accton’s P/E multiple given lower revenue share 
in global network/switching market and lower EPS CAGR in 2026-28E (Exhibit 4). 
We use 2028 as the valuation base year to capture various future AI-related business 
opportunities which have been monitored and discussed by investors, including new 
CPO/cooling/rack business and new customers (CSPs, enterprises, start-ups). Our US 
analyst Ruplu Bhattacharya also uses 2028 as the valuation base year for Celestica. 
In terms of P/E, Accton currently trades at 18x one-year forward P/E, 17x 2027E P/E, or 
12x 2028E P/E, vs. the historical P/E trading average of 11x since 2008 when the 
company started making profits (Exhibit 8). Its 2027E and 2028E P/E are both below 
overall peers’ average of 22x and 18x, respectively (Exhibit 11). 
In terms of P/B, Accton currently trades at 8.7x one-year forward P/B, 8.1x 2027E P/B, or 
5.4x 2028E P/B, above the historical trading average of 3.3x since 2007 (Exhibit 9). Its 
2027E P/B of 8.1x is above overall peers’ average of 6.4x, while 2028E P/B of 5.4x is 
below overall peers’ average of 6.4x. 
In terms of PEG, Accton’s valuation looks attractive, with 0.29 PEG and 54% ROE in 
2028E, vs. overall peers’ average of 0.73 PEG and 30% ROE (Exhibit 10).

### Page 9

Business overview: Switch for datacenter 
as key driver amid AI uptrend 
Accton established a strong position in white-box datacenter switching during the 2010s 
and has since become a key supplier to hyperscalers. According to third parties’ data 
(including Qyresearch and Intelmarket), white-box datacenter switching TAM is expected 
to grow to US$7.8-8.1bn in 2032-34 from US$3.2-3.5bn in 2025 with an around 13% 
CAGR during the period. Accton has been the second largest white-box datacenter 
switching vendor following Celestica. We noted Accton’s rising revenue share in global 
datacenter switching market, especially for top-4 US cloud datacenter switching market.  
According to Dell’Oro, Accton had 7.0% of revenue share in global datacenter switching 
market in 2Q26, vs. 7.0%/5.6% in 1Q26/2Q25 and Celestica’s 15.1% in 2Q26. In top-4 
US cloud datacenter switching market, Accton’s revenue share increased to 14.1% in 
2Q26, vs. 13.3%/12.6% in 1Q26/2Q25 and Celestica’s 36.6% in 2Q26. Its revenue share 
in tier-2, neo and other cloud also gradually increased to 3.1% in 2Q26, vs. 2.4%/2.8% in 
in 1Q26/2Q25. Within that hyperscale environment, Accton is also catching up with 
Celestica and OEM peers in front-end and AI back-end data center switching market. See 
more details from our US team’s latest Switching Trend 2Q26 report published on 7 
October 2026.  
We see the Switch business for datacenter as the key sales/earnings driver for Accton 
amid AI uptrend. We expect the Switch business (including datacenter and non-
datacenter like enterprise, campus, telecom) to contribute 54-68% of total sales in 
2026-28. Switch for datacenter will become the biggest sub-segment in 2027-28 at 53-
61% of total sales, vs. 18-42% in 2025-26, surpassing Network Application business. 
Switch has historically been Accton's core business, while Network Application business, 
including AI accelerator cards, smart NICs, and other customized networking platforms, 
has become increasingly important amid strong hyperscaler AI investments. Metro 
Access Switch and Wireless are less meaningful in the future, in our view. Sales from 
Other Services should be relatively volatile as NRE sales are included (Exhibit 7). 
We also see Accton’s product portfolio increasingly aligned with AI datacenter 
infrastructure spending. Beyond switch and networking platforms, Accton is expanding 
into optics, liquid cooling, and rack-level integration, allowing it to capture a larger 
portion of system value within AI deployments. Management has highlighted optical 
technologies and liquid-cooled rack-scale solutions as key areas of development, in 
addition to AI accelerator modules and high-speed switches.

Network Application business: AI platform deployments also drive growth 
Accton's Network Application business includes AI accelerator cards, smart NICs, 
network interface products and customized networking and computing platforms. The 
segment expanded rapidly in 2023-25, with revenue contribution increasing to 62% from 
23%, thanks to strong demand for AI-related accelerator cards and customized 
datacenter platforms for hyperscalers’ AI deployments. The growing adoption of custom 
accelerators by hyperscalers creates attractive opportunities for ODM/JDM suppliers. As 
cloud customers seek to optimize performance and total cost of ownership, demand for 
customized hardware platforms, system integration and rack-level solutions has 
increased alongside investments in AI infrastructure. 
We expect Network Application business to continue growing but at a more normalized 
pace after the initial AI-card ramp. As data center switch demand accelerates, the faster 
growth of the switch business should increasingly outweigh the contribution from 
Network Application to Accton’s overall revenue growth, making Switch Accton’s largest 
business segment.

### Page 12

AI networking architecture: scale-up, 
scale-out, scale-across; CPO/optical as 
future trend 
Modern AI infrastructure relies on three complementary networking domains: scale-up 
connects accelerators within a node or tightly coupled rack domain, scale-out across 
racks and within a cluster/datacenter, and scale-across between clusters/datacenters. 
Each domain has different bandwidth, latency, and network requirements, but all three 
are becoming increasingly important as AI infrastructure grows in scale, complexity, and 
rising density.

Exhibit 18: AI infrastructure scales from accelerator connectivity within a node or rack, to rack-to-rack networking within a cluster/datacenter, and 
ultimately to cluster/datacenter interconnection 
Scale-up, scale-out, and scale-across in AI networking architecture

Source: BofA Global Research, Broadcom 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Scale-up: rising demand for intra-system connectivity 
Scale-up provides tightly coupled connectivity among accelerators within a node or rack-
scale computing domain. These networks require extremely high bandwidth and ultra-
low latency to support accelerator-to-accelerator communication and collective 
computing operations. Nvidia’s NVLink currently dominates commercial deployments, 
while Ethernet and UALink are gaining momentum as hyperscalers increasingly deploy 
custom and merchant accelerators. According to Dell’Oro Group, scale-up networking 
TAM is expected to be more than double for switches deployed in AI back-end networks 
and should account for more than half of the AI back-end switch market by 2030.

### Page 13

Scale-up interconnects remain primarily copper-based currently as copper offers low 
latency, good signal integrity, and high stability in short distance (e.g. below 10 meters) 
with favorable cost and power characteristics. Our supply chain check suggests that 
optical connectivity may begin penetrating scale-up more meaningfully after 2H27 as 
rising bandwidth requirements push copper closer to its physical transmission limits. 
For Accton, its scale-up exposure appears to be developing into an incremental business 
line. We see its business with the biggest CSP customer has expanded into scale-up 
switch trays, in addition to its existing scale-out switches and AI cards used in compute 
trays.

Scale-out: the core architecture for AI cluster expansion 
Scale-out expands computing capacity by connecting more servers and racks within an 
AI cluster, typically through high-speed Ethernet or InfiniBand fabrics. In common leaf-
spine architecture, leaf or top-of-rack (ToR) switches connect compute racks, while spine 
switches provide the high-speed backbone between leaf switches. Scale-out is a well-
established architecture for AI cluster expansion and remains a major driver of AI back-
end networking demand. See more in Appendix: What is leaf-spine architecture? 
We believe scale-out represents Accton’s largest and most immediate AI networking 
opportunity. Larger clusters require more leaf and spine switches, higher port speeds, 
and greater switching capacity, supporting the demand for Accton’s 400G/800G/1.6T 
Ethernet platforms. Scale-out is also the primary near-term application for CPO-enabled 
Ethernet switches, as CPO can help address power, thermal, and signal-integrity 
constraints at higher switch bandwidths.

Scale-across: extending AI networking beyond individual 
clusters/datacenters 
Scale-across connects separate AI clusters or datacenters, potentially across different 
buildings or locations, to support distributed computing and resource pooling. As power, 
cooling, and space constraints limit expansion within individual facilities, AI 
infrastructure begins to extend across multiple sites/locations, increasing demand for 
high-capacity and predictable inter-datacenter connectivity.  
While commercial adoption remains at an early stage, scale-across represents the 
potential market for Accton beyond conventional Ethernet switching, particularly in long-
reach optical networking and datacenter interconnection. The company has been 
developing relevant optical-switching technologies and has used its OWS technology in 
Taiwan-Japan cross-border IOWN field validation over real computing resources and 
network infrastructure. We see this development/project as early evidence of Accton’s 
system-integration capabilities and potential role in future scale-across applications.

CPO: the potential next-gen architecture for AI switches 
AI workloads are driving rapid increases in networking bandwidth and power 
consumption as infrastructure scales across scale-up, scale-out, and scale-across 
domains. The tougher requirement has led to bottlenecks in conventional electrical 
interconnects. We believe the transition toward optical networking could open new 
growth opportunities for Accton, where its exposure spans different layers of the optical 
networking stack, including CPO (co-packaged optics) for high-speed switch 
interconnects, OCS (optical circuit switch) for direct optical connectivity, and OWS 
(optical wavelength switch) for wavelength-level optical networking and DCI (Datacenter 
Interconnection).

### Page 14

The rapid growth of AI, cloud, and HPC workloads is driving exponential increases in 
datacenter computing and networking requirements. As AI clusters scale, traditional 
electrical interconnects are increasingly constrained by power consumption, signal 
integrity, and bandwidth density. Even pluggable optical transceivers are gradually 
approaching their physical and thermal limits at 800G and higher speeds. 
To overcome these challenges, the industry is actively developing co-packaged optics 
(CPO), which integrate switch ASIC and optical engine within a single package, replacing 
traditional pluggable transceivers. By significantly shortening the electrical path between 
ASIC and optical engine, CPO can reduce power consumption and latency while 
improving signal integrity and bandwidth density. As Ethernet networking migrates from 
400G to 800G, 1.6T, and eventually 3.2T, CPO theoretically becomes a key technology 
enabling the next-generation AI data-center networks.

Exhibit 19: CPO integrates optical engine and switch ASIC within a single package 
Potential key trend of optical transceiver packaging in high-end data center

Source: BofA Global Research, IDTechEX 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

In terms of addressable market, our US team expects CPO adoption to gain traction 
from 2027. CPO switch penetration is limited as of aggregate switch market today, with 
deployments largely limited to engineering samples. However, CPO is expected to begin 
scaling with more meaningful penetration after 2028. Our US team expects CPO switch 
TAM to reach US$24.4bn in 2030 from US$0.4bn in 2026, with 14-43% penetration in 
2028-30, vs. 1-5% in 2025-27 (Exhibit 20). See our global team’s US Semiconductors: 
Scaling AI with Photons: Primer on Optical Interconnects published on 9 March 2026.

### Page 15

Exhibit 20: Our US team views CPO is at the early stage now but expects a CPO switching TAM to 
reach US$24.4bn in 2030 (with 43% penetration) from US$0.4bn in 2026 (with 3% penetration) 
CPO switching market size (in US$bn) and penetration, 2024-2030E

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

For Accton, while commercialization remains at an early stage, we believe Accton's early 
investment in optical integration technologies positions the company favorably to 
benefit from the industry's migration toward CPO-based networking architectures. 
The company has been investing in optical integration technologies for several years, 
positioning itself for the industry's transition from pluggable optics toward more 
integrated optical architectures. The company demonstrated an on-board optical engine 
solution as early as 2020 and has gradually expanded its networking portfolio from 400G 
to 800G and now 1.6T-class platforms. 
We highlight Accton's latest 102.4T Ethernet switch, based on Broadcom's Tomahawk 6 
ASIC (Exhibit 21). The platform supports both conventional OSFP1600 pluggable optics 
and a CPO configuration. Under the CPO architecture, the optical engine is integrated 
directly adjacent to the switch ASIC, helping mitigate power, thermal, and signal-
integrity challenges associated with ultra-high-bandwidth networking.

### Page 16

Beyond the 102.4T platform, Accton continues to advance toward more tightly 
integrated optical architectures. In 2025, its subsidiary Edgecore demonstrated a proof-
of-concept CPO solution integrating optical engines and switch silicon within a single 
package, highlighting the group's long-term commitment to CPO development and next-
generation optical networking. 
We believe CPO could provide Accton with several strategic benefits. First, CPO 
increases the optical content per switch, creating opportunities for higher-value switch 
platforms compared with conventional pluggable architectures. Second, Accton's 
established position as a leading networking switch ODM, combined with its capabilities 
in switch design, optical integration, thermal management and system-level integration, 
could allow the company to capture a larger portion of the AI networking value chain as 
CPO adoption increases. Finally, the company’s optical subsidiaries and growing 
ecosystem partnerships could further strengthen its ability to deliver integrated optical-
networking solutions.

Potential optical technologies under watch: OCS and OWS 
Although CPO significantly shortens the electrical path between switch ASIC and optical 
engine, data traffic may still require optical-electrical-optical (O-E-O) conversion when it 
traverses packet switches. As AI clusters continue to expand, the growing volume of data 
traffic increases network latency, power consumption, and switching overhead, creating 
bottlenecks beyond the switch level.  
To address these challenges, the industry is increasingly exploring optical switch, which 
enables optical signals to remain in the optical domain while traversing the network. 
Compared with traditional Ethernet switching, optical switching reduces intermediate 
packet-processing stages and minimizes O-E-O conversions, potentially lowering power 
consumption and latency while improving network efficiency. Two complementary 
optical switching approaches are Optical Circuit Switching (OCS) and Optical Wavelength 
Switching (OWS). While OCS establishes dedicated optical paths between endpoints, 
OWS provides more granular control by dynamically routing individual wavelengths 
carried within an optical fiber. Our check suggests that OCS is currently for customized 
adoption only and OWS is at the initial/validation stage. The initial shipment/sales for 
Accton's OCS and OWS products may start from 2027.

OCS: enabling direct optical connectivity for AI clusters  
Unlike traditional Ethernet architectures, OCS establishes dedicated optical paths 
between endpoints without processing individual packets. When two endpoints need to 
communicate, a controller dynamically configures the switch matrix to create a 
dedicated optical circuit between them. By enabling direct optical connectivity, reducing 
intermediate switching stages, and minimizing O-E-O conversions, OCS can lower 
latency and power consumption while improving network efficiency, providing a high-
bandwidth optical layer for inter-rack and inter-cluster connectivity. Currently the largest 
OCS use case is from Google (MEMS-based). See more OCS introduction in our global 
team’s US Semiconductors: Scaling AI with Photons: Primer on Optical Interconnects 
published on 9 March 2026.

### Page 17

Exhibit 22: OCS creates direct optical connections, reducing intermediate packet-switching stages and O-E-O conversions 
Optical Circuit Switch diagram and example (MEMS-based example from Google)

Source: BofA Global Research, Google 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Our US team expects OCS market to expand to US$4.3bn by 2030 from US$1.0bn in 
2025, driven by accelerating adoption in AI and HPC networks (Exhibit 23). Despite an 
expected decline in ASPs as volumes scale, shipment growth is projected to remain 
robust, increasing to 140K units by 2030 from around 24K units in 2025.

Exhibit 23: Our US team expects OCS TAM to exceed US$4bn by 2030 with robust shipment growth

expertise in LCoS-based (liquid crystal on silicon based) optical switching technologies, 
while Accton's experience in hyperscale networking and switch design provides a 
pathway toward system-level deployment.

We believe Accton's position in OCS is different as it focuses on complete optical switch 
systems rather than individual optical components. While Taiwan's CPO ecosystem is 
mature across foundry, packaging, and optical components, the local supply chain for 
OCS system remains at an early stage. Accton is among the few Taiwanese networking 
companies directly developing OCS platforms, creating an opportunity to extend from 
conventional packet switching into the optical switch layer of future AI networks. Our 
supply chain check suggests that OCS products are in sampling and pilot-production 
stages. Combined with its expertise in high-speed Ethernet switches, optical integration, 
and rack-level infrastructure, we believe Accton could benefit from an early-mover 
advantage as the optical switch ecosystem develops in the coming years.

OWS: enabling flexible wavelength-level optical switching 
While OCS establishes dedicated optical circuits between endpoints, Optical Wavelength 
Switching (OWS) provides more granular control by routing individual wavelengths 
within an optical fiber. Through wavelength-division multiplexing (WDM), multiple 
wavelengths can be transmitted simultaneously over a single fiber. OWS dynamically 
directs these wavelengths to different destinations, allowing optical bandwidth to be 
allocated more efficiently without processing traffic in the electrical domain or relying 
on repeated O-E-O conversions.

Exhibit 24: By controlling the phase of incident light at the pixel level, 
LCoS enables dynamic wavelength routing and forms the core 
technology behind many wavelength-switching systems 
LCoS modulates and steers individual optical wavelengths

Source: FOSCO 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Exhibit 25: Incoming wavelengths are separated, dynamically 
redirected by the LCoS processor, and routed to different output ports 
without O-E-O conversion 
LCoS-based OWS architecture

Source: FOSCO  
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Accton's OWS development has reportedly progressed beyond the laboratory stage and 
is moving toward customer validation and commercialization through its subsidiary 
Edgecore. The company's flagship OWS platform, the IRX3032, supports 32 WDM 
channels, up to 800G coherent optics, and up to 25.6Tbps of optical bandwidth while 
operating entirely in the optical domain. According to Edgecore, the platform delivers 
160ns end-to-end latency and supports point-to-point, ring, and full-mesh network 
topologies. The IRX3032 has also reportedly entered validation with a US CSP and NTT 
from Japan, suggesting continued progress toward commercial deployment. 
Beyond standalone switch, Accton is positioning OWS as part of its broader all-
photonics networking strategy. The IRX3032 has been incorporated into IOWN 
(Innovative Optical and Wireless Network) architecture, where optical switching is 
combined with high-speed Ethernet switches, transport equipment, and AI infrastructure. 
More importantly, Accton's OWS technology has already been used in Taiwan-Japan

### Page 19

cross-border validation based on real computing resources and network infrastructure, 
supporting applications such as real-time translation and AI-enabled traffic analytics. In 
our view, Accton's key advantages in OWS should be its ability to combine optical switch 
technology and established networking system experiences, via InLC's LCoS technology, 
Edgecore's OWS platform, and its expertise in high-speed Ethernet switches and system 
integration. Although the OWS market remains at an early stage, growing interest from 
hyperscalers and telecom operators in all-optical networking architectures could create 
new opportunities beyond conventional packet switching.

that supports 32 WDM to deliver ultra-low-latency optical routing

Key peer comparison: Celestica (CLS US) 
Celestica (CLS US; covered by Ruplu Bhattacharya) is a leading EMS company based in 
North America. Celestica provides design, engineering, manufacturing, and hardware-
platform solutions to OEMs and cloud service providers. Celestica was incorporated in 
Ontario, Canada in 1996. Prior to that, the company was an IBM manufacturing unit that 
provided manufacturing services to IBM for more than 75 years. It was acquired by Onex 
Corporation and Celestica management in 1996 and went publicly listed in 1998. 
Headquartered in Toronto, it operates across Americas, Europe, and Asia with around 45 
sites globally. It is also expanding capacity in Texas (US) through a new campus. See 
also: Ruplu Bhattacharya’s latest Celestica report published on 29 July 2026.

Celestica’s overlap with Accton from key CCS segment 
Celestica has two business units: CCS (Communications, Server, Storage) and ATS 
(Aerospace & Defense, Industrial, Capital Equipment, and HealthTech). The CCS segment 
is more relevant to Accton’s business scope. As of 1H26, CCS contributed 81% of total 
sales and the remaining 19% came from ATS.

### Page 22

Ongoing R&D efforts from both Accton and Celestica 
Accton’s R&D expenses had been around 2x of Celestica in absolute terms over 2015-
1H26. Accton’s R&D expenses to sales were 3-5%, vs. 0-1% for Celestica under 
Celestica’s larger sales base (Exhibit 34). We believe both companies are putting R&D 
resources into AI rack-scale infrastructure-related business and more design-
/engineering-led solution business.

### Page 24

Financial analysis: 37%/42% sales/EPS 
CAGR in 2026-28 
We project 37%/42% sales/EPS CAGR for 2026-28. Our 2026-28 EPS estimate is 3-5% 
above consensus (Exhibit 39). Our key assumptions are the following: 
• Sales: We estimate 2026-28 sales to grow 35-67% YoY, mainly driven by strong AI 
market demand and ongoing spec/platform migration. We also reflected 3Q26 
preliminary sales of NT$120.7bn (+26% QoQ, +66% YoY) into our forecast. Its 
monthly sales sustained at historical high levels in 3Q26 with solid YoY trend, 
indicating still robust market demand amid component shortage (Exhibit 40 and 
Exhibit 41). Our 3Q26 EPS forecast is 2% above consensus (Exhibit 42). 
• Margins: We project 2026-28 GM of 19.3-20.1% and OPM of 14.8-16.1%, with 
improving product mix, rising sales scale, and stringent opex control. We also take 
high component cost into consideration. 
• Non-operating items, tax rate, and share count for EPS: We follow the 
historical patterns for non-operating items and tax rate. We also assume no capital-
raising plan in 2026-28.

### Page 26

Balance sheet: net cash position and high ROE 
Accton has maintained a net cash position since 2007. We believe it reflects the robust 
cash-generation capability, supported by sustained profitability and healthy free cash 
flow. As of 2Q26, its net cash stood at NT$21.5bn or 2% of its market cap, vs. 3% in 
1Q26, 2-8% in 2017-25, and 17-51% in 2007-16 (Exhibit 46). Accton had a higher net 
cash to market cap% before 2016 as its market cap jumped significantly from 2017.  
Accton’s cash conversion cycle (CCC) days have been low at nearly or below two months. 
It was 31 days in 2Q26, vs. 15 days in 1Q26 and 11-63 days in 2007-25 (Exhibit 47). We 
attribute the low CCC days to long A/P days, with A/R days and inventory days sustained 
at stable levels. We believe its CCC days reflected efficient working-capital 
management, faster inventory turnover amid robust AI networking demand, strong 
bargaining power as networking ODM leader, and mature supply chain management. 
In terms of DuPont analysis, we expect Accton’s ROE to remain high at 45-50% in 2026-
28E (Exhibit 48) with continued net margin expansion (Exhibit 49) and lower financial 
leverage and asset turnover. We view the ROE sustainability as a reflection of a strong 
balance sheet with retained earnings accumulation and cash build-up.

Cashflow analysis: negative OCF in 1H26 for higher 
working capital; higher capex for global expansion 
Accton has generated positive FCF in most of the years since 2007 (Exhibit 50), 
supported by generally positive OCF and relatively disciplined capex. It had FCF outflow 
of NT$19.1bn in 1H26, with OCF outflow of NT$15.5bn (for higher working capital) and 
high capex of NT$3.7bn (for ongoing capacity expansion). We believe the higher working 
capital in 1H26 was for strategic raw material procurement/preparation amid supply 
shortage and cost inflation. Historically in 2007-25, its FCF ranged from outflow of 
NT$1.3bn to inflow of NT$30.0bn, with OCF inflow except 2008 and 2017.  
In 2026-28, we expect Accton to generate FCF inflow of NT$38.6-89.7bn, supported by 
strong profitability, which more than offsets the higher working capital and capex 
required for global capacity expansion.  
Looking at capex, Accton’s annual capex has accelerated from 2023, reaching NT$2.2-
4.9bn during 2023-2025, vs. NT$112-952mn in 2007-2022 (Exhibit 51). The increase 
primarily reflects the company’s multi-year global capacity expansion plans and R&D 
efforts. We forecast capex to remain at a historically high NT$6.0-7.0bn annually in 
2026-28E, mainly supporting new capacity in Vietnam, Singapore, Malaysia, and Dallas 
(US), as well as continued expansion/renewals for the existing facilities. 
As for financing activities, historically Accton has undertaken limited external financing 
with only CB issuances. No capital raising or capital reduction was conducted. As of end-
2025, no corporate bond was outstanding. We thus assume no capital-raising plan in 
2026-28. We believe bank borrowing will be the key method for financing needs.

Ongoing capacity expansion in Taiwan and overseas 
Accton currently has 4 sites in Taiwan, 3 in US, 1 in Vietnam and 1 in Netherlands 
(Exhibit 51). The company continues to expand its global manufacturing network to 
support local demand. Based on our checks, five sites are under expansion now. 
In Taiwan, production at Taoyuan facility started ramp-up in 3Q26. Hsinchu site is being 
converted into a new production line. In August 2026, Accton announced to rent lands in 
Nanliao (南寮) in Hsinchu with around 30,865.90 pings (or 102,036 square meters). We 
believe its Taiwan facilities will be more dedicated to high-end AI products.  
Overseas, Accton also announced to rent lands and buildings in Vietnam and Singapore. 
It plans to add the third manufacturing facility in Vietnam, to ramp its Dallas (US) site 
for rack-level business, and to commence production at new facilities in Singapore and 
Malaysia. We believe Malaysia will be a key hub for cooling products from 2027, vs. 
optical/CPO in Singapore.

Company Description 
Accton is a leading Taiwan-based networking/switch white-box ODM/JDM/solution 
provider. Its product offering includes datacenter/server switch, enterprise switch, metro 
Ethernet switch, smart network interface card, router, 5G gateway, edge-computing 
server, and wireless networking equipment. Accton is also expanding into high-speed 
optics, liquid cooling, and rack integration for next-generation AI infrastructure.  
Accton was founded on 9 February 1988 and went publicly listed on Taiwan Stock 
Exchange on 15 November 1995. The company has operations/facilities across Taiwan, 
US, Southeast Asia, and Europe, with ongoing capacity expansion, and its headquarter is 
in Zhubei, Taiwan.

Management team 
Kuo-Hsiu Huang (黃國修), Chairman 
Kuo-Hsiu Huang has served as the Chairman of Accton Technology since 2024 and the 
Director since 2014. Currently, he also holds key positions in several subsidiaries, 
including the Chairman of Accton Tech (China) Co., Ltd., E-Direct Corporation, and MuXi 
Technology (Nanjing) Co., Ltd., as well as the Director of Accton Century Holding (BVI) 
Co., Ltd., Accton Asia Investments Corp., Accton Investment Corporation, and Edgecore 
Networks Singapore Pte. Ltd. In addition, he serves as the Chairman of Golden Tengis 
Co., Ltd. and Kuan Xin Investment Co., Ltd. Previously, he served as an Assistant Manager 
of Accton Technology. Mr. Huang holds an MBA from Peking University. 
Chiu-Hsia Wei (韋秋霞), Vice Chairman 
Chiu-Hsia Wei has served as the Vice Chairman of Accton Technology since 2024. 
Currently, she is also the Director of two subsidiaries, Accton Logistics Corporation and 
Accton Global Inc. In addition, she serves as the Legal Representative of Kuan Xin 
Investment Co., Ltd. Previously, she served as the Senior Associate Vice President of 
Procurement at Accton Technology. Ms. Wei graduated from Shih Chien Junior College. 
Jackal (Hsun-Te) Li (李訓德), President 
Jackal Li has served as the President of Accton Technology since 2025. Currently, he also 
serves as the Director of several subsidiaries, including Accton Logistics Corporation, 
Accton Global, Inc., Vietnam Accton Technology Co., Ltd., and Accton Manufacturing and 
Service, Inc. Mr. Li holds a Bachelor’s Degree in Business Management from Chung Hua 
University. 
Fanny (Fang-I) Chen (陳芳儀), Chief Finance Officer 
Fanny Chen has served as the CFO of Accton Technology since 2023. Currently, she also 
serves as the Supervisor of several subsidiaries, including Edgecore Networks 
Corporation, E-Direct Corporation, and GoldiLink Technology Corp. Previously, she served 
as the Accounting Manager and Assistant Manager of Accton Technology. Ms. Chen 
holds a Bachelor of Science in Finance and Taxation from National Chengchi University. 
Edward (Hsing-Hsuan) Lin (林信玄), Corporate Governance Manager 
Edward Lin has served as the Corporate Governance Manager of Accton Technology 
since 2021. Currently, he also serves as the Chief Legal Officer and the Spokesman of 
the company. Mr. Lin holds a Master of Science in Technology Law from National Yang 
Ming Chiao Tung University.

Appendix 
What is leaf-spine architecture? 
Leaf-spine is a modern data center network architecture designed to provide high 
bandwidth, low latency, and predictable performance, especially for workloads that 
generate heavy server-to-server (east-west) traffic such as AI training, cloud services, 
and distributed computing. In a leaf-spine design, the network is simplified into two 
layers: the leaf layer and the spine layer. Every leaf switch connects to every spine 
switch, creating a full-mesh topology between the two layers (Exhibit 57). 
The leaf switches sit at the edge of the network and connect directly to servers, storage 
systems, and other endpoints. The spine switches act as the backbone, interconnecting 
all leaf switches but never connecting directly to endpoints. As a result, traffic between 
any two servers attached to different leaf switches always traverses the same number 
of hops: Leaf → Spine → Leaf. This predictable path helps maintain consistent latency 
and simplifies network scaling. 
Leaf-spine architecture became popular because traditional three-tier networks (access, 
distribution, core) were optimized for north-south traffic between users and servers. 
Modern datacenters, however, generate significant east-west traffic, where servers 
communicate heavily with one another. AI clusters are a prime example, as GPUs 
frequently exchange data during model training. A leaf-spine fabric minimizes 
bottlenecks by enabling multiple equal-cost paths and load-balancing traffic across all 
available links.

### Page 32

Another major advantage is scalability. As capacity requirements grow, operators can 
add more leaf switches to connect additional servers, or add more spine switches to 
increase backbone bandwidth. As every leaf connects to every spine, the architecture 
scales in a modular and predictable way without requiring a significant redesign. The 
flexibility serves as one of key reasons behind hyperscale cloud providers and AI 
infrastructure operators’ wide adoption in leaf-spine topologies. 
Leaf-spine is particularly important because it drives demand for high-speed Ethernet 
switching (100G, 400G, 800G and beyond). AI datacenters often dedicate large numbers 
of spine and leaf switches to provide the massive bandwidth required by GPU clusters, 
making the leaf-spine fabric a foundational building block of modern AI infrastructure.

Exhibit 57: Leaf-spine is a modern data center network architecture designed to provide high bandwidth, low latency, and predictable performance, 
especially for workloads that generate heavy server-to-server (east-west) traffic such as AI training, cloud services, and distributed computing

### Page 34

Price objective basis & risk 
Accton (XTWOF) 
Our PO of NT$3,200 is based on 18x 2028E EPS. We use 18x P/E multiple (around 1SD 
above historical P/E average since 2008) in view of strong AI uptrend, leading position in 
AI networking/switch white-box, expanding OPM with sustainable/high GM, and double-
digit sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-28E.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) faster 800G/1.6T deployment by key customers, (2) 
easing component supply shortage, (3) new customer/project win, (4) accelerating 
optical/cooling business development, (5) faster capacity expansion progress, (6) 
favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD).

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) AI product/project delay and slower 800G/1.6T 
adoption, (2) key customers' demand push out due to component supply shortage, (3) 
higher GM pressure due to component cost inflation, (4) pricing pressure and/or 
market/order share loss due to peer competition, (5) slower capacity expansion, (6) 
unfavorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) or macro uncertainty.

Industry View In-Line
At the rack level, Maia 300 is the key incremental story, while
Nvidia/AMD exhibits offered limited new system-level hardware.
Nvidia’s MGX component upgrades are the main supply-chain
watch points, although PTFE and next-gen interconnect and
cooling connector (ZQD) adoption remains uncertain.

Nvidia/AMD –  focus on ramps rather than new platforms. Most ODM displays
were similar to Computex Taipei (early June), so timing of ramp is key. For Nvidia,
VR200 racks have started a small-volume ramp, with the Nvidia Groq 3 LPX rack
following in late 2026/early 2027 and standalone Vera CPU racks in late 1Q27/early
2Q27, based on our checks. For AMD, Helios remained largely unchanged with
recent checks, with racks ramping in 1Q27, with pilot-build observations pointing to

Shennan Circuits for EAM PCBs and Fositek/Parker for quick disconnects.
Maia 300 -  a larger in-house inference opportunity. Microsoft plans production
deployments in 2027 as it scales Maia toward multi-gigawatt deployments. Each
rack integrates 96 accelerators within 167 kW. Based on that, each gigawatt of
deployment would imply 5 to 5.5K racks, and we believe Hon Hai and Wiwynn could
be the ODM partners, while our pilot-build observations point to ISU Petasys for the

accelerator PCB, AVC for fans, and Fositek/Staubli for quick disconnects.
PTFE - technical upside, but no confirmed timing of adoption. Nvidia is evaluating
PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon following the Kyber delay and showcased its
NVLink PTFE PCB with seven suppliers (though we think there are suppliers missing
from this list). Better signal performance and reduced reliance on Q glass are
potential benefits, but processing complexity, higher costs and yield/qualification

risks remain hurdles to commercial adoption.
Ultra Dense Interconnect - next-generation interconnect opportunity for FIT.
Nvidia showcased six suppliers for this next-generation interconnect, which is
intended to replace Amphenol’s current HD2 connector in midplane and cable
cartridge applications. However, we believe adoption is still about two years away. In
the meantime, the supply chain is working to improve yields and bring ASPs below
US$100, with pogo-pin supply currently a bottleneck. Our checks identify FIT as the

key design partner for Kyber’s planned board-to-board connector, a position that
bodes well for its supply share once the product ramps.
ZQD - higher flow density and an expected ASP premium. Nvidia’s new ZQD
targets higher flow rates per unit area and was displayed with 14 suppliers.
Although the supply chain is working to improve yields and lower costs, greater
design complexity should keep ASPs above current QD pricing of US$20–30.

and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Major Takeaways from OCP Summit 2026
ODM/OEM: Almost all the ODM/OEMs had a booth at OCP, but during our visit, we feel
most of the products were largely similar to what was showcased at Computex, such as
the Vera Rubin NVL72 rack, the LPX tray, the Vera standalone tray. So net, we think from a
hardware perspective, there was nothing new on Nvidia or AMD. We believe the Nvidia
VR200 rack is already starting ramp in small volume, with the LPX rack starting to ramp

later this year or early next year, and the Vera standalone CPU rack ramping late 1Q27 or
early 2Q27.
AMD: Wiwynn, Meta, and AMD each showcased the Helios rack, which was unchanged
from last year’s OCP display with one exception: this year’s version featured the actual
compute tray in place of last year’s dummy tray, allowing a closer look. That said, the
compute tray itself was not new to us, as we had already seen it at OCP Taiwan in August.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Shennan Circuits is one

of the pilot build PCB suppliers for the MI455 Helios EAM PCB.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek (MQD02) and
Parker (UQD04) are one of the pilot build quick disconnect connector suppliers for
the MI455 Helios compute tray.
• HPE displayed the Helios switch tray at the OCP Summit. We believe the other
supplier of the Helios switch trays is Celestica.
Microsoft: Maia 300 represents a major expansion of Microsoft’s in-house inference

infrastructure. Announced on October 12, 2026, with production deployments planned for
2027, it forms part of Microsoft’s commitment to scale the Maia family to multi-gigawatt
deployments and make it the primary inference platform for its products and services. The
design targets long-context reasoning, conversational AI, and multi-step agentic
workloads, with an emphasis on improving inference economics through coordinated
silicon, software, and system design.

Maia 300 rack integrates 96 accelerators within a 167 kW power envelope, providing
approximately 27.6 TB of aggregate HBM capacity. There are 4 accelerators per tray,
implying 24 compute trays per rack, including six high-radix switch ASICs that are
integrated into three liquid-cooled switch trays, providing the rack-local connectivity
required by all 96 accelerators. Microsoft projects 40% more tokens per megawatt than
leading GPU-based systems and up to 7× Maia 200’s rack-level token throughput, though

this takes into account that the Maia 300 rack  includes twice as many accelerators per
rack.
• The Official Microsoft Blog A two-tier Ethernet scale-up network connects up to
128 racks, or 12,288 accelerators, while liquid cooling removes more than 80% of
rack heat. Each compute tray includes 8x fans  (Exhibit 3 ), and based on our
observations at the OCP Summit, we believe AVC is one of the pilot build fan
suppliers for the sample compute tray.

• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe ISU Petasys is one of
the pilot build PCB suppliers for Microsoft’s Maia 300 AI accelerator.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek and Staubli are
pilot build MQD03 (quick disconnect connector) suppliers for the compute tray.

Nvidia MGX Ecosystem
PTFE: We previously wrote that our supply chain checks suggest that Nvidia had initially
planned to adopt PTFE CCL for the very high-layer-count midplane in its Kyber
architecture. With Kyber now delayed, and Rubin Ultra no longer expected to use that
architecture, as previously discussed, the focus has shifted back to the Oberon
architecture. Nvidia is again evaluating PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon, and at this year’s

OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE PCB, with a total of 7 suppliers, including
VGT, TTM, Taiflex, SYTECH, Shennan Circuits, Kinwong, and Flexium (Exhibit 4  & Exhibit
5 ). However, our checks indicate that actual adoption for Rubin Ultra remains uncertain.
However, we find the supplier list puzzling. Our checks suggest that WUS PCB and
Unimicron are also working on this solution, and that Flexium, which has collaborated
with other PCB suppliers, may handle only a few process steps. Our checks also indicate

that Shennan Circuits, WUS PCB and VGT are now the leading PCB makers for this
solution, with relatively higher yields.
Advantages of PTFE
• Best signal performance: the lowest Dk and Df of the mainstream options, below
even standard M9-class CCLs. This matters most where insertion-loss budgets are
tightest, i.e. the highest speeds and highest layer counts.
• Stable electricals: the low Dk/Df hold up across frequency and temperature, and
PTFE absorbs very little moisture, so performance is stable in use.

• Less dependence on Q glass: because PTFE’s intrinsic dielectric constant is already
very low, reaching the target Dk requires less (and in some hybrid constructions,
none) of the expensive, supply-constrained Q glass. That is both a cost lever and a
supply-security benefit.
Disadvantages of PTFE

### Page 5

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 5
• Mechanically soft: low modulus makes it difficult to handle and to hold to
tolerance.
• Difficult lamination: PTFE does not flow like a thermoset, so bonding needs either
very high temperatures or lower-melting fluoropolymer (such as PFA
(polyfluoroalkoxy))/thermoset bond layers; its high, non-linear CTE stresses
copper adhesion and complicates layer-to-layer registration, and pressing typically
requires vacuum or an inert atmosphere with longer cycles.

• Difficult drilling: the soft, gummy resin tends to smear and can tear, and any
ceramic filler is abrasive on tooling. Also, the hole wall will not accept electroless
copper without an added activation step (sodium etch or plasma) on top of normal
desmear.
• Higher cost and qualification risk: materials and fabrication are both more
expensive, the qualified supply base is narrower, and yields at very high layer
counts carry risk.
Exhibit 4: Nvidia's NVLink PTFE Board

Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.

Exhibit 5: Nvidia PTFE PCB Supplier List

Ultra Dense Interconnect: Currently, Nvidia's NVL72 racks are using Amphenol's Paladin
HD2 connectors for VR200 NVL72 midplanes and cable cartridges. Our previous checks
done around Computex 2026 in June, indicate ASP could reach US$70-90+ per set. For
the next-generation Kyber racks, our checks have previously indicated that a new midplane
connector called "Voronoi" will be introduced, and FIT is the key design partner for this

new board-to-board connector. At this year’s OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased a new
connector it calls the “Ultra Dense Interconnect”, with six suppliers in total. This was our
first in-person look at the design. We believe adoption is still two years out, and the
supply chain is currently focused on improving yields and optimizing costs to bring the
ASP below US$100. Our checks suggest that pogo pin supply is currently a bottleneck for
this connector.

### Page 7

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Exhibit 6: Nvidia next-generation Ultra Dense Interconnect
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
ZQD: At COMPUTEX in Taipei earlier this year, we noted that our checks indicated Nvidia
was planning to introduce a new quick disconnect (QD) design for next-generation server
racks to achieve higher flow rates per unit area as cooling requirements rise. At this year’s
OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased that design, called ZQD, with 14 suppliers in total. Our

checks indicate the supply chain is also working to improve yields and optimize costs to
bring ZQD ASPs down. However, given the greater complexity of the design, we expect
ZQD ASPs to remain above current QD pricing of US$20–30 per set.

## Document 193: 9月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ ～FRBは年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ   ～FRBは年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

FED  Watching

2026 年 10 月 8 日

1/5 
9 月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ 
～FRB は年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治  
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・9 月FOMCでは全会一致（12 対 0）で 0.25%の利上げを決定。非投票メンバーを含む参加者 
全 19 人が利上げに賛成した。 
・経済・金利見通し（SEP）提出者の大半が年内（10 月または 12 月）の更なる利上げを見込 
んでおり、全体としてタカ派的トーンが際立つ。 
・インフレリスクは上振れ傾向にあるものの、中長期のインフレ予想は 2%目標水準に安定。 
FRBは毎会合のデータ次第で柔軟に判断する「オープンな姿勢」を維持している。 
・今後の見通しとしては、景気や労働市場が安定する中でコアCPIやコアPCEの鈍化が確認され 
れば、予防的な追加利上げが見送られる可能性がある（最終決定は 12 月FOMCまでのデータ次
第） 。

１． 9 月FOMC ：参加者 19 人全員 が利上げ に賛成  
ケビン・ウォーシュFRB議長の新体制下で初めての利上げが決定された 9 月FOMCの議事録が公表 
された。新体制では会合直後の声明文のテキスト量が大幅に削減されたほか、記者会見時間も短
縮され、直接的な情報発信量が絞り込まれている。そうした中で、後日公表される「議事録」の
情報量は引き続き高水準に維持されており、FRBの本音を推し量る最重要ツールとしての重みが増
している。ただし、公表がFOMC終了から 3 週間後となるため、情報の遅効性には注意が必要であ 
る。 
9 月の利上げ決定については、投票権のないメンバーも含めた参加者全員（19 人）が利上げに賛
成した。さらに、経済・金利予測（SEP：議長自身は今回も SEP を提出していない）で示されていた 
通り、SEP を提出した 18 人の参加者のうち大半が「年内（10 月 27-28 日または 12 月 15-16 日）に
更なる利上げが適切となる可能性が高い」と見解を示しており、インフレへの強い警戒感を示すタ 
カ派（金融引き締め寄り）なトーンが際立つものとなった。ただし、委員会として今後の政策判断 
は新たに入手されるデータに依存（Data-dependent）するスタンスを明確に維持している。

２．労働市場は完全雇用水準で安定、インフレには上振れリスク 
委員会は全会一致（12 対 0）で、FF金利誘導目標レンジを 0.25％ポイント引き上げ、3.75％〜

### Page 2

2/5 
4.00％とすることを決定した。労働市場が完全雇用に近い水準で安定している一方で、インフレ 
率が高止まりしていることが背景にある。 
インフレの判断では、参加者は共通して、 「インフレ率は高止まりしており、ここ数ヶ月でイン 
フレ抑制に向けた十分な進展は見られていない」と指摘した。 「原油や精製燃料製品の価格を押し 
上げた継続的な地政学的動向や、急増するAI関連投資がインフレ圧力に寄与している」との認識 
が示された。 
複数の参加者がコア財・コアサービス価格の上振れや、関税の影響に言及したほか、数名の参 
加者がPCE指数の算出手法改定による影響や、3 ヶ月年率換算のインフレ率が年後半にインフレを 
過小評価しやすい傾向に留意すべきだと警鐘を鳴らした。一方で参加者全員が、 「中長期のインフ 
レ予想に関する市場ベースおよび調査ベースの指標が、委員会の 2％目標と一致する水準を維持し 
ている」と判断しており、追加利上げを過度に急ぐ必要性は示されなかった。 
労働市場に関しては、参加者は概ね「労働市場の状況が安定している」と判断し、 「完全雇用に 
近い状態にある」と評価した。 「失業率が現在の水準付近にとどまり、労働市場の状況は引き続き 
安定する」と予想した。一方で、数名の参加者が低水準の採用・解雇率など流動性の低下を指摘 
し、一部の参加者がAI専門職における局所的な賃金上昇圧力に触れた。そのうえで、参加者は概
ね「労働市場に対する上振れリスクと下振れリスクはほぼ均衡している」と評価した。 
経済活動に関しても、参加者は一般に「堅調なペースで拡大している」と評価。 「地政学的動向 
による供給面での悪影響が強まったものの、旺盛な設備投資と底堅い個人消費が経済活動を支え 
ている」との判断が示された。なお、一部の参加者からは、AI関連投資が設備投資と潜在生産性 
を押し上げる一方で中期的に総需要が総供給を上回るリスクや、株高などを背景とする金融環境 
の緩和的状況、高所得層と低・中所得層における消費の二極化構造への懸念が指摘された。 
以上のように、参加者は共通して「労働市場が完全雇用に近く力強さの兆候も見られる一方で 
インフレ率は高止まりしており、経済活動が堅調なペースで拡大していること」を強調した。さ
らに、ほぼすべての参加者が、 「インフレリスクは上振れに傾いているものの、労働市場に対する 
リスクは減少し、現在はほぼ均衡している」と評価した。こうした経済見通しと変化するリスク 
バランスに基づき、全参加者（19 人）がFF金利誘導目標レンジの引き上げが適切であると判断し
た。

3．政策金利の水準感とリスク管理の姿勢 
今回の議論において注目されるのは、利上げの根拠と政策金利水準に対する見解の多様さであ 
る。多くの参加者は、予想以上の需要や供給面での悪影響によりインフレ率が持続的に目標を上 
回り続けることに対する保険（Insurance）を提供するため、誘導目標レンジを高めの軌道とする 
ことがリスク管理上の観点から賢明であると強調した。 
一方で、相当数の参加者はリスク管理上の観点からではなく、自身の標準的な見通し（Modal 
outlooks）に基づいて高めの目標軌道が必要であると見なした。さらに、数名の参加者は中立金 
利の推計値を引き上げたことに触れ、現在の政策金利は「緊縮的ではない、あるいは極めて緩や

までにFF金利誘導目標レンジを再度引き上げることが適切となる可能性が高いとの見方を示し 
た。ただし、全員が共通のスタンスとしてオープンな姿勢で各会合に臨み、今後の決定は新たに

毎会合ごとに判断されることを示し、政策の自由度を維持した。 
今回の議事録は全体として「年内追加利上げ」に前向きなタカ派的トーンが目立ったものの、 
インフレ予想の安定や「毎会合データ次第」というスタンスから、FRBが無理な連続利上げを焦っ 
ているわけではないことが示された。今後の金融政策の焦点を左右するのは、10 月・12 月FOMCま
でに発表される景気、労働市場や、コアCPI、コアPCEの動向である。景気や労働市場の安定が続 
くと予想されるもとで、インフレの鈍化傾向が確認される場合、予防的な追加利上げは見送られ 
る可能性がある（最終決定は今後のデータ次第） 。

(market: 4.4%, ING: 4.4%). This brings the year-to-date GDP growth

By industry, China's tertiary sector continued to grow fastest for
the sixth consecutive quarter, up 5.2% YoY in 3Q, versus 3.6% and

With a few months left in the year, we believe China's economy is
well positioned to achieve its 2026 growth target of 4.5-5.0%.
However, the economy remains quite unbalanced heading into
2027, leaving growth vulnerable to disruptions in external demand.

Retail sales growth was stronger than forecasts (market: 0.5%,
ING: 0.6%), rising to 1.1% YoY from 0.4%. Strong
communication appliance (31.1%) sales drove the data, likely
beneﬁting from a host of new phone model launches. Beyond
this category, sales of consumer staples such as tobacco and
alcohol (13.8%), grains & oils (6.7%), and beverages (5.0%) all
outperformed. However, many discretionary consumption
categories, such as gold and jewellery (-9.6%) and autos

(-21.2%), remained weak.
Fixed asset investment growth stabilised at -7.2% YoY after
decelerating every month since March, coming in stronger than
expectations (market: -7.3%, ING: -7.5%). This stabilisation
reﬂects public-led (-3.2%) and manufacturing (-2.1%) FAI
picking up on the month, oﬀsetting slowdowns in private-led
(-10.4%), real estate (-20.6%), and infrastructure (-4.2%) FAI.
Investment has clearly been a major drag this year, so
stabilisation is encouraging, possibly as eﬀorts to accelerate

ﬁscal spending outlined at July's Politburo meeting begin to
take eﬀect.
Industrial production continues to be the main outperformer,
up 5.5% YoY from 5.2% in August, in line with market
expectations (market: 5.5%, ING: 5.5%). Hi-tech manufacturing
(15.3%) remains a bright spot, and export-oriented industries
such as computers and communications (16.4%), rail, ships,
and aerospace (11.7%), and autos (6.7%) continue to
outperform.
70-city property prices continued to drop, but by less than

what we saw in August's data. New home prices (-0.14% MoM)
fell the least in 17 months. Prices stabilised or rose in 21 cities.
Existing home prices (-0.26% MoM) saw the smallest drop in 4
months, with prices stabilising or rising in 11 cities. Tier 1 cities
continued to perform well on the month, with a few tier 2 cities
joining the price recovery.
Compared with rapid export growth, domestic activity continues to
lag. But September's data at least shows modest positive

momentum heading into 4Q26.

## Document 294: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

September 2026: Marked improvement in
services business confidence
September 2026: Business climate in the
manufacturing sector comfortably in the
expansion zone for the 8th month in a row

pressures
September 2026: Slight further increase in

September 2026: Marked increase in inflation,
to a 3-year high
Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022.
